        file: arxiv.tex
        from: 7283
        to: 7292
      hash: sha256:637fa11b844d14113004ac1d55960b1ce79391fb2c260e464e9bff40912cfa1a
      minhash: 4mu0BJWLcrcB5cCgYKortbyi4oRmGRcbm2aCqnW+t0VxA4PWP0jnP0YYpGNL/vJOcoEH0gzoS6Pxbmu9yOt0yOKChwTxepZ0lib3mNZw3EBLPrMMNPVl8tVL8VeB0qvjLzrn0KVNWrcwR/5U4gAlK6e37Ik+u7BQyh6rHTGhMUi8qBVZbzNfBsVy/tBL5x33zmuA8hHgvB94wEAT4Qlkb1ABkh8t+Y0V7XH3HzjEeWPu4l/sisF5fM3z6StMq4rGfD6x+VREIY4uU9xzaLJcFLLT2Y1EwbOpRqneHAshEJDGOkdgJPEGdyvrZJOG38MWLB7HRuxtWjsYcbuIlQFUTg==
      census: 2609.34339v2#env-45
      extractor: ax/v0.5.32
---
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{statement:always-unilateral walk covering S}
	\label{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal exploration}
	Let \(D\) be an always-unilateral temporal digraph on \(n\) vertices and
	let \(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\) be a set of size \(k\).
	If \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi \geq \sum_{i=2}^k	\ifx\relax n,i\relax		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}	\else		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}	\fi ∈ 	\ifx\relax kn + k^2\relax		O	\else		\mathchoice{O( kn + k^2 )}{O( kn + k^2 )}{O( kn + k^2 )}{O( kn + k^2 )}	\fi\), then there is a temporal walk \(W\) in \(D\)
	such that 
	\(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax W\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( W )}{V( W )}{V( W )}{V( W )}	\fi\).
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:always_path_maxdegree_3}
    For every positive integer $n'$, there exists an always-unilateral temporal digraph $D$ of order $n\geq n'$ such that each snapshot of $D$ is a directed path, $\Delta(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi) \leq 3$, $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi\geq \frac{1}{128} n^2$, and $D$ does not admit any temporal exploration.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{theorem:tournament-exploration-upper-bound}	Let \(D\) be a semicomplete temporal digraph of order $n\geq 2$. If $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi\geq \lfloor \frac{4}{3}n - 1\rfloor$, then \(D\) admits a temporal exploration.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{statement:tournament-exploration-lower-bound}
	For every integer \(n\geq 2\) there exists a temporal tournament \(D\) of order \(n\) with $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi=\lfloor \frac{4}{3}n-2\rfloor$ that does not admit any temporal exploration.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:np_hardness}
	\TemporalExploration/ remains \NP/-hard even if the input $D$ is a temporal tournament and $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi =|V(D)|-1$.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{statement:semicomplete approximation hardness}
    For every $\epsilon>0$, \TemporalExplorationFastest/ cannot be approximated within a factor $(\frac{4}{3}-\epsilon)$, even when restrained to temporal tournaments, unless \P/ = \NP/.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}
    For every integer $c$, every always-strong temporal digraph \(D\) on \(n\) vertices
	with \(\ensuremath{\bar{\@empty{\delta}}(D)} \leq c\)
	and \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi \geq 	\ifx\relax n,c\relax		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}}}	\else		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}}}( n,c )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}}}( n,c )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}}}( n,c )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:degree-n-c-upper-bound-strong}}}( n,c )}	\fi ∈ 	\ifx\relax cn\relax		O	\else		\mathchoice{O( cn )}{O( cn )}{O( cn )}{O( cn )}	\fi\) admits a temporal exploration.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{statement:degree-n-c-lower-bound}
	For every \(c \geq 1\) and every \(n \geq c+1\) there is 
	an always-strong temporal digraph \(D\) on \(n\) vertices
	where	\(\ensuremath{\bar{\@empty{\delta}}(D)} \leq c\), \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi = c(n - c - 2)\), and
	\(D\) does not admit a temporal exploration.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- observation-1: body_md -->
\label{statement:strong-temporal-u-v-path}
	Let \(D\) be an always-strong temporal digraph.
	Let \(u,v ∈ 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\).
	If \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi ≥ n - 1\),
	then there is a temporal \(u\)-\(v\)-path in \(D\).
<!-- end observation-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Let $D=(D_i)_{i\in [t]}$.
	For every \(i ∈ [t - 1]\),
	if \(v ∉ 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}	\fi\),
	then \(\left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right| > \left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right|\)
	as \(D_i\) is strongly connected.
	Since \(\left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right| \geq 2\),
	after \(n - 1\) steps, we have
	\(\left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right| = \left| 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi \right|\),
	in which case \(v ∈ 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi\).
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{statement:unilateral layers -> temporally unilateral}
	Let $D$ be an always-unilateral temporal digraph of order $n\geq 2$.
	If $\ell(D)\geq 2n-3$, then for every \(u,v \in 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\), there is
	a temporal \((u,v)\)-walk or
	a temporal \((v,u)\)-walk in \(D\).
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $D=(D_i)_{i\in [t]}$ and \(u,v \in 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\) be distinct.
    Assume for a contradiction that \(u\notin 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}	\fi\) and \(v\notin 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi\).

	Let \(1 \leq i \leq 2n - 4\).
	If there is directed path from $u$ to $v$ in \(D_{i + 1}\),
	then there is some arc \(ww' \in A(D_{i+1})\) 
	such that \(w \in 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}	\fi\) and \(w' \notin 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}	\fi\). Hence, \(w' \in 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}	\fi\) and \(\left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right| > \left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}{R_{i}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right|\). 
    By symmetry, if there is a directed path from $v$ to $u$ in \(D_{i + 1}\), then \(\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right| > \left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right|\). 
	Thus, at least one of the sets \(	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( u )}	\fi, 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i+1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i+1}^{D}( v )}	\fi\) is larger than the corresponding set at step \(i\).
    Therefore,  we have
    \[
        |	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi| + |	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}	\fi| \geq |	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}	\fi| + |	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}	\fi| + t-1.
    \]
	Observe that, if \(\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right| = 1\), then \(\left| 	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}	\fi \right| \geq 2\), as
	there can be at most one sink in an unilateral digraph. Hence,
    \[
        |	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi| + |	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}	\fi| \geq |	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}{R_{1}^{D}( u )}	\fi| + |	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}{R_{1}^{D}( v )}	\fi| + t-1 \geq t+2 \geq 2n-1.
    \]
    Therefore, one of the sets $	\ifx\relax u\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}{R_{t}^{D}( u )}	\fi, 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}{R_{t}^{D}( v )}	\fi$ has size $n$, a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{statement:lower bound:unilateral layers -> temporally unilateral}
	For every integer \(n \geq 3\) there is an always-unilateral temporal digraph \(D\) of order $n$ with $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi = 2n-4$ containing two vertices \(u,v \in 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\)
	such that
	no temporal $u$-$v$- or $v$-$u$-walk exists in \(D\).
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let \(V = \{v_{1}, v_{2}, \ldots, v_{n}\}\).
	For every \(1 \leq i \leq n - 2\), let $D_{2i-1}$ consist of the directed path $(v_1, v_2, \ldots, v_n)$ and let $D_{2i}$ consist of the directed path $(v_n,v_{n-1},\ldots,v_1)$.
	Clearly, each $D_i$ is unilateral, and there is no temporal \((v_1,v_n)\)- or \((v_n,v_1)\)-path in \(D=(D_i)_{i\in [2n-4]}\).
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{statement:no growth implies containment}
	Let \(D\) be an unilateral digraph and \(A, B ⊆ 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\).
	If \(\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{}(A)}= \ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{}(B)} = \emptyset \), then \(A ⊆ B\) or \(B ⊆ A\).
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Assume towards a contradiction that \(A\) and \(B\) are incomparable with respect to \(⊆\).
		Choose \(x \in A \setminus B\) and \(y \in B \setminus A\).
		Since \(D\) is unilateral,
		there is a directed path \(P\) from \(x\) to \(y\), or from \(y\) to \(x\) in \(D\).
		In the first case, all vertices of \(P\) are inside \(A\), so
		\(y\in A\), a contradiction. The second case similarly gives
		\(x\in B\). Hence \(A\) and \(B\) are comparable with respect to \(⊆\).
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{statement:reachability growth}
	Let \(D\) be an always-unilateral temporal digraph on $n$ vertices,
	\(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\), \(S' ⊆ S\), and let \(d_0 \leq d_1\) be integers.
	If, for some integer $t'$, \(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax S'\relax		R_{t'}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t'}^{D}( S' )}{R_{t'}^{D}( S' )}{R_{t'}^{D}( S' )}{R_{t'}^{D}( S' )}	\fi\),
	\(\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t'}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right| \geq d_0\) for all \(v ∈ S'\),
	\(\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{t'}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}{R_{t'}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right| \geq d_1\) for some \(v ∈ S'\), and
	\(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi \geq t' + 2n - 1 - (d_1 + d_0)\),
	then there is some \(v^* ∈ S'\)
	such that
	\(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax v^*\relax		R_{}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}	\fi\).
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $D=(D_i)_{i\in [t]}$.
    Let \(M_{t'-1} = S'\).
	For each \(i ∈ [t',t]\), 
	let \(M_i \subseteq M_{i-1}\) be a minimal set
    such that
    \(S \subseteq 	\ifx\relax M_i\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( M_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( M_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( M_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( M_i )}	\fi\).
    Furthermore, let
	\(d_{0,i} = \min_{v ∈ M_i}\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right|\),
	\(d_{1,i} = \max_{v ∈ M_i}\left| 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi \right|\), and
	\(d_{+,i} = d_{0,i} + d_{1,i}\).

	If \(M_i = \{v^*\}\) for some \(i ∈ [t',t]\),
	then \(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax v^*\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{i}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{i}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{i}^{D}( v^* )}	\fi\) and we are done.
	If \(d_{+,i} \geq 2n - 1\) for some \(i ∈ [t',t]\),
	then \(d_{1,i} \geq n\), and
	so there is some \(v ∈ M_i\)
	such that
	\(	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi = 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\) and we are done.

	Otherwise, let \(v_1 ∈ M_i\) be a vertex
	such that
	\(\left| 	\ifx\relax v_1\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i}^{D}( v_1 )}	\fi \right| = d_{1,i}\).

	If \(\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_{i}}(	\ifx\relax v_1\relax		R_{i - 1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v_1 )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v_1 )}	\fi)} = \emptyset\),
	then by \cref{statement:no growth implies containment} and by definition of \(M_i\), we have
	\(\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_{i}}(	\ifx\relax v'\relax		R_{i - 1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v' )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v' )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v' )}{R_{i - 1}^{D}( v' )}	\fi)} \neq \emptyset\)
	for every \(v' ∈ M_i ∖ \{v_1\}\).
	In particular, \(d_{0,i} > d_{0,i - 1}\) and,
	thus, \(d_{+,i} > d_{+,i-1}\). 

	Otherwise, we have \(d_{1,i} > d_{1,i-1}\) and
	again \(d_{+,i} > d_{+,i-1}\).

	Let \(t'' = t' + 2n - 1 - (d_1 + d_0)\).
	The argumentation above implies that \(d_{+,t''} \geq 2n - 1\),
	and so \(d_{1,t''} \geq n\), or
	\(M_{t''} = \{v^*\}\).
	In both cases, as argued above, we obtain the claimed statement.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root} 
	Let \(D\) be an always-unilateral temporal digraph of order \(n\) and $S\subseteq V(D)$ with \(\left| S \right|=k\).
	If \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi \ge 	\ifx\relax n,k\relax		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}	\else		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,k )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,k )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,k )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,k )}	\fi \coloneqq 2n - k - 1\),
	then there exists \(v^* \in S\) such that $S\subseteq	\ifx\relax v^*\relax		R_{}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{}^{D}( v^* )}	\fi$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Let $D=(D_i)_{i\in [t]}$ and $|S|=k$.
	Write \(S=\{s_1,\ldots,s_k\}\).
	Given a set \(A ⊆ S\) and a time step \(i ∈ [t]\),
	define
	\(w_i(A) = \left| 	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi \right|\).

	We iterate for \(i ∈ [t]\), constructing sets \(U'_i ⊆ U_i ⊆ S\)
	such that \(U_i\) is a minimal set satisfying \(U_i' \subseteq U_i\) and
	\begin{equation}
		S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax U_i\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( U_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( U_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( U_i )}{R_{i}^{D}( U_i )}	\fi,
		\tag{C1}
		\label{eq:C1}
	\end{equation}
	for every non-empty set \(A \subseteq U_i'\), we have
	\begin{equation}
		w_i(A) \geq i + \left| A \right|,
		\tag{C2}
		\label{eq:C2}
	\end{equation}
	and
		\begin{equation}
			\label{eq:I1}
			\left| U_i' \right|+\mu_i\ge k,
			\tag{C3}
		\end{equation}
	where \(\mu_i:=\min_{v \in U_i}w_i(\{v\})\).

	Start by setting \(U_0 = U_0' = S\).
    Clearly, \eqref{eq:C1}, \eqref{eq:C2} and \eqref{eq:I1} hold for these sets.
    Moreover, by definition of $	\ifx\relax U_0\relax		R_{0}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{0}^{D}( U_0 )}{R_{0}^{D}( U_0 )}{R_{0}^{D}( U_0 )}{R_{0}^{D}( U_0 )}	\fi$, the set \(U_0\) is a minimal set satisfying \eqref{eq:C1}. 

	On step \(i\),
	stop the construction if 
    \(\left| U_{i-1}' \right| = 1\). or
        We say that a non-empty set \(A ⊆ U'_{i-1}\) is \emph{bad}
	if \eqref{eq:C2} does not hold for \(A\) on step \(i\).
	As \eqref{eq:C2} holds in time step \(i - 1\), every bad set \(A\) satisfies
	\[
		w_{i-1}(A) = i - 1 + \left| A \right|
		\quad\text{and}\quad
		\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_{i}}(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}	\fi)}
		= \ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_{i}}(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi)} =
		\emptyset.
	\]

	By \cref{statement:no growth implies containment}, for two distinct bad sets \(A, B\)
	we have that the sets 
	\(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi\) and
	\(	\ifx\relax B\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}	\fi\)
	are comparable with respect to \(⊆\).
    We show that $A$ and $B$ are also comparable with respect to~\(⊆\).
	Without loss of generality, we have
	\(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi ⊆ 	\ifx\relax B\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}	\fi\).
	The other case follows analogously.

	Assume towards a contradiction that there is some \(v ∈ A ∖ B\).
	Let \(B' = B ∪ \{v\}\).
	Then, since $v\in A$ and \(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi ⊆ 	\ifx\relax B\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}	\fi\), we have
	\(	\ifx\relax B'\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}	\fi = 	\ifx\relax B\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}	\fi\),
	which implies that
	\(\left| 	\ifx\relax B\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}{R_{i}^{D}( B )}	\fi \right| = \left| 	\ifx\relax B'\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}{R_{i}^{D}( B' )}	\fi \right| \geq i - 1 + \left| B' \right| = i + \left| B \right|\),
	a contradiction to the assumption that \(B\) is bad.

	Thus, the family of bad sets forms a chain with respect to \(⊆\).
    If there are no bad sets, we choose \(U_i' = U_{i-1}'\).
	Otherwise, let \(A_0\) be the unique minimal bad set and choose \(v_0^i ∈ A_0\) arbitrarily.
	We set \(U'_i = U'_{i - 1} ∖ \{v_0^i\}\).
	As every bad set in \(U'_{i-1}\) contains \(v_0^i\),
	there are no bad set in \(U'_i\),
	satisfying \eqref{eq:C2} on step \(i\).

	Finally, choose \(U_i ⊆ U_{i - 1}\) as a minimal subset
	containing \(U'_i\)
	such that \eqref{eq:C1} holds.

	We now prove that \eqref{eq:I1} holds on step \(i\).
	Observe that \(\mu_{i-1} \leq \mu_{i}\), since
	\(	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}	\fi ⊆ 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi\) for any vertex $v$.

	Assume towards a contradiction that \eqref{eq:I1} does not hold.
	Since \(\left| U'_i \right| \geq \left| U_{i-1}' \right| - 1\) and
	\(\left| U'_0 \right| = k\),
	we have \(\left| U_i' \right| \ge k - i\), and
	hence \(\mu_i<i\).
	Since \eqref{eq:I1} holds at time \(i-1\) and
	\(\mu_i\) is non-decreasing, the failure at
	time \(i\) must occur because \(\left| U'_i \right| = \left| U'_{i-1} \right| - 1\) and
	\(\mu_i=\mu_{i-1}\).

    Let \(v ∈ U_i\) such that \(w_i(\{v\}) = \mu_i\).
	It cannot be the case that \(v\in U_{i-1}'\), as
    \[
        w_{i-1}(\{v\}) \leq w_{i}(\{v\}) = \mu_i < i = (i-1)+|\{v\}|
    \]
    would contradict \eqref{eq:C2} for $i-1$.
    		    Hence $v\in U_i \setminus U_{i-1}'$.
			    As \(\mu_i = \mu_{i-1} \leq w_{i - 1}(\{v\}) \leq w_i(\{v\}) = \mu_i\),
    we have that \(w_{i-1}(\{v\}) = w_i(\{v\}) = \mu_i\).
	Therefore, \(\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_i}(	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}	\fi)} = \emptyset\).
                                	
	Since \(\left| U'_i \right| <  \left| U'_{i-1} \right|\), 
	there is some bad set \(A ⊆ U'_{i-1}\) at step \(i\).
	As argued before, \(\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_i}(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}	\fi)} = \emptyset\).
	By \cref{statement:no growth implies containment},
	\(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi\) and \(	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi\) are comparable with respect to \(⊆\).

                    
    As \(v \notin U_{i-1}'\), by minimality of \(U_{i-1}\)
    there is some \(y_v \in 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}	\fi\)
    such that
    \(v\) is the only vertex in \(U_{i-1}\)
    with
    \(y_v \in 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( v )}	\fi\),
    as otherwise we could remove \(v\) from \(U_{i-1}\) and
    still satisfy \eqref{eq:C1} at step \(i - 1\).
    As \(A \subseteq U_{i-1}' \subseteq U_{i-1}\),
    we have that \(y_v \notin 	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i-1}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}{R_{i-1}^{D}( A )}	\fi = 	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi\).
    Hence, \(	\ifx\relax A\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}{R_{i}^{D}( A )}	\fi \subseteq 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{i}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}{R_{i}^{D}( v )}	\fi\).
    This implies that \(w_i(\{v\}) \geq w_{i-1}(A) \geq i\),
    a contradiction to \(w_i(\{v\}) = \mu_i < i\).
 	Hence, \eqref{eq:I1} holds on step \(i\).

	Let \(t'\) be the number of iterations above.
    Assume towards a contradiction that \(t' \geq n\).
    Then, because \eqref{eq:C2} holds at step \(n\),
    we have \(w_{n}(U_{n}') \geq n + \left| U_{i}' \right| > n\), a contradiction.
	Hence, \(t' \leq n - 1 \leq 	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi\) and
    the iteration stops when \(\left| U_{t'}' \right| = 1\).

	Let \(\{u\} = U_{t'}'\).
	Then \eqref{eq:C2} gives \(w_{t'}(\{u\}) \ge {t'}+1\).
	By \eqref{eq:I1}, we also have \(\mu_{t'}\ge k-1\).
	Hence, there is some \(v_1 ∈ U_{t'}\)
	such that
	\(w_{t'}(\{v_1\}) \geq t' + 1\) and
	for every \(v ∈ U_{t'}\)
	we have \(w_{t'}(\{v\}) \geq k - 1\).
	As \(t \geq 2n - k - 1 = t' + 2n - 1 - (t' + 1 + k - 1)\),
	we can apply \cref{statement:reachability growth} with
    \(S' = U_{t'}\),
    \(d_1 = t' + 1\) and
    \(d_0 = k - 1\),
	obtaining some \(v^* ∈ U_{t'} \subseteq S\)
	such that
	\(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax v^*\relax		R_{t}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{t}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{t}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{t}^{D}( v^* )}{R_{t}^{D}( v^* )}	\fi\), as desired.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Let \(\ell_i = 	\ifx\relax n,i\relax		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}	\else		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\@empty{t}_{\ref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root}}}( n,i )}	\fi = 2n - i - 1\), 
	\(t_{k+1} = 0\) and, for $2\leq i\leq k$,
	\(t_i = \sum_{j=i}^k\ell_j\).
	Thus
	\begin{align*}
		t_{2} & = \sum_{i=2}^{k}\ell_i = (2n - 1)(k - 1) - \sum_{i=2}^ki 
		\\[0em] & = (2n - 1)(k - 1)- k(k + 1)/2 - 1
		\\[0em] & = 2kn -(k^2 + 3k)/2 - 2n \leq 	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi.
	\end{align*}
	For each \(2 \leq i \leq k\),
	let \(H_i = D[1 + t_{i + 1}:t_i]\).
	Note that \(	\ifx\relax H_i\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( H_i )}{\ell( H_i )}{\ell( H_i )}{\ell( H_i )}	\fi = \ell_i\) and
	that \(H_{i+1}\) occurs before \(H_i\) along the snapshots of \(D\).

	Set \(S_k = S\).
	Iterate for \(i = k\) to \(2\) as follows.
	On step \(i\), by \cref{statement:unilateral layers -> temporal root},
	there is some \(v_i ∈ S_i\)
	such that 
	\(S_i ⊆ 	\ifx\relax v_i\relax		R_{\ell_i}^{H_i}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{\ell_i}^{H_i}( v_i )}{R_{\ell_i}^{H_i}( v_i )}{R_{\ell_i}^{H_i}( v_i )}{R_{\ell_i}^{H_i}( v_i )}	\fi\).
	Set \(S_{i - 1} = S_i ∖ \{v_i\}\). 
	If \(i < k\),
	let \(W_i\) be a temporal \(v_{i+1}\)-\(v_{i}\)-walk in \(H_{i+1}\).
	Because \(v_i ∈ S_i ⊆ S_{i+1}\), such a walk exists.

	After completing the iteration above, we obtain temporal paths \(W_{k-1}, W_{k-2}, \ldots, W_{1}\).
	Let \(W = W_{k-1} · W_{k-2} · \ldots · W_{1}\).
	It is immediate by construction that \(S ⊆ 	\ifx\relax W\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( W )}{V( W )}{V( W )}{V( W )}	\fi\).
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{statement:degree-three-lower-bound}
	For every integer \(n' \geq 1\) there is an always-strong temporal digraph \(D\) of order \(n\geq n'\) such that $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi$ has maximum degree $3$, \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}{\ell( D )}	\fi \geq \frac{1}{9}n^2\), all snapshots of $D$ are equal, and \(D\) does not admit any temporal exploration.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For each \(i ∈ [n']\) let \(P_i\) be the directed path \((u_i, v_i, w_i)\).
	Let \(C\) be the cycle \((v_{n'}, w_{n'}, w_{n' - 1}, \ldots,\allowbreak w_{1}, u_{1}, u_{2}, \ldots, u_{n'}, v_{n'})\).
	Let \(D'\) be the digraph consisting of the union of all \(P_i\)'s and \(C\), see \cref{fig:strong-degree-three-lower-bound} for an illustration of the construction.
    
    \begin{figure}
    	\centering
    	\begin{tikzpicture}
    		\node[vertex] (v1) at (0,0) {};
    		\node[left =0.0mm of v1] (v1l) {$v_1$};
    		\node[vertex] (v2) at (1,0) {};
    		\node[left =0.0mm of v2] (v2l) {$v_2$};
    		\node[vertex] (v3) at (2,0) {};
    		\node[left =0.0mm of v3] (v3l) {$v_3$};
    		\node[vertex] (w1) at (0,1) {};
    		\node[above =0.0mm of w1] (w1l) {$w_1$};
    		\node[vertex] (w2) at (1,1) {};
    		\node[above =0.0mm of w2] (w2l) {$w_2$};
    		\node[vertex] (w3) at (2,1) {};
    		\node[above =0.0mm of w3] (w3l) {$w_3$};
    		\node[vertex] (u1) at (0,-1) {};
    		\node[below =0.0mm of u1] (u1l) {$u_1$};
    		\node[vertex] (u2) at (1,-1) {};
    		\node[below =0.0mm of u2] (u2l) {$u_2$};
    		\node[vertex] (u3) at (2,-1) {};
    		\node[below =0.0mm of u3] (u3l) {$u_3$};
    		\path[directededge] (u1) to (v1);
    		\path[directededge] (v1) to (w1);
    		\path[directededge] (u2) to (v2);
    		\path[directededge] (v2) to (w2);
    		\path[directededge] (u3) to (v3);
    		\path[directededge] (v3) to (w3);
    		\path[directededge] (u1) to (u2);
    		\path[directededge] (u2) to (u3);
    		\path[directededge] (w3) to (w2);
    		\path[directededge] (w2) to (w1);
    		\path[directededge, bend right = 70] (w1) to (u1);
    		\path[directededge, dashed, color=blue] 
    		(2.2,-0.8) to      		(2.2,0) to         		(2.2,0.8) to       		(-1.0,0.8) to      		(-1.0,-0.8) to     		(0.2, -0.8) to     		(0.2, 0.6) to      		(-0.8, 0.6) to      		(-0.8, -0.6) to     		(1.2, -0.6) to
    		(1.2, 0.0);
    	\end{tikzpicture}
    	\caption{\label{fig:strong-degree-three-lower-bound}
    	The digraph \(D'\) constructed in the proof of \cref{statement:degree-three-lower-bound} for \(n' = 3\).
    	A Hamiltonian walk of length 10 is given by the dashed path.
    	}
    \end{figure}

	We first prove that a shortest Hamiltonian walk \(W\) in \(D'\) has length at least \(\ell \coloneqq (n')^2\).
	Let \(W_{i,j}\) be a subpath of \(W\) from \(v_i\) to \(v_j\) which is internally disjoint from \(V\coloneqq \{v_1,\dots,v_{n'}\}\).
	Clearly, \(D'\) contains only one possibility for \(W_{i,j}\), visiting \(w_i\) to \(w_{1}\) and then \(u_{1}\) to \(u_j\).
	Hence, \(W_{i,j}\) has length \(i + j + 1\).

	Let \(v_a\) be the first vertex of \(V\) in \(W\) and let \(v_b\) be the last.
	Let \(V' = V \setminus \{v_a, v_b\}\).
	For each vertex \(v_i ∈ V'\), there are two subpaths \(W_{j,i}\), \(W_{i,k}\) in \(W\),
	one from some \(v_j\) to \(v_i\) and one from \(v_i\) to some \(v_k\).
	The length of \(W\) is thus at least 
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_{v_i ∈ V'}(2i + 1) + a + b + 1 = \sum_{v_i ∈ V}(2i + 1) - a - b - 1 & = 2\sum_{i=1}^{n'}i + n' - a - b - 1\\[0em]
		& = n'(n' + 1) + n' - a - b - 1.
	\end{align*}
    
	This value is minimized when we choose \(a = n'\) and \(b = n' - 1\), yielding the length of
	\(n'(n' + 1) + n' - 2n' = (n')^2\) for \(W\).

	Let \(D\) be the temporal digraph consisting of \(\ell - 1\) snapshots, all equal to \(D'\).
	It is clear from the definition of temporal walks that
	a temporal exploration of \(D\) corresponds to a Hamiltonian walk for \(D'\)
	of length \(\ell - 1\).
	However, as proven above, there is no Hamiltonian walk of length \(\ell\) in \(D'\).
	Hence, \(D'\) does not admit a temporal exploration.

	Since \(D\) contains \(n \coloneqq 3n'\) vertices, we have that \(\ell = n^2/9\).
	Further, it is immediate from the construction that maximum degree of \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi\) is 3,
	as every snapshot is the same and has maximum degree 3.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lemma:lower_bound_path_apex}
    For every integer $n\geq 0$, there exists a temporal digraph $H$ on $n+1$ vertices $v_0,\dots,v_n$ such that:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $	\ifx\relax H\relax		\ell	\else		\mathchoice{\ell( H )}{\ell( H )}{\ell( H )}{\ell( H )}	\fi= \frac{1}{2}n(n+1)-1$;
        \item each snapshot of $H$ is a directed path;
        \item for every $j\geq 1$, $v_j$ has degree at most $3$ in $	\ifx\relax H\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}	\fi$, and $v_j$ is adjacent to $v_0$ in $	\ifx\relax H\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}	\fi$; 
        \item $v_0$ is adjacent to $v_1$ in every snapshot of $H$; and
        \item $H$ does not admit any temporal exploration. 
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Let us define a temporal digraph $H$ as follows. 
    Let $V(H) = \{v_0,v_1,\ldots,v_n\}$. For every $i\in [n]$, let $D_i$ be the digraph consisting of the directed path
    \[
        (v_{i-1},v_{i-2},\ldots,v_0,v_{i},v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n).
    \]
    We let $H$ be the temporal digraph consisting of $i$ copies of $D_i$ for every $i\in [n-1]$ and $n-1$ copies of $D_n$ (where the copies of each $D_i$ appear consecutively and in the natural ordering $D_1,D_2,\ldots,D_n$). See Figure~\ref{fig:quadratic_bound_paths_unb_degree} for an illustration.

    \begin{figure}
    \centering
    \begin{tikzpicture}
        \newcommand{0.449}{1.1}
                \begin{scope}
            \node[] at (-0.449,0) {$D_1:$};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_0$] (a1) at (0,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_1$] (a2) at (0.449,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_2$] (a3) at (0.449*2,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-2}$] (a3b) at (0.449*4,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-1}$] (a4) at (0.449*5,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i}$] (a5) at (0.449*6,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{n-1}$] (a5b) at (0.449*8,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_n$] (a6) at (0.449*9,0) {};
            
            \node[] at (0.449*10,0) {$\times 1$};

                        \draw[directededge] (a1) -- (a2);
            \draw[directededge] (a2) -- (a3);
            \draw[directededge] (a3) -- (0.449*2.7,0);
            \draw[thick,dotted] (a3) -- (a3b);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*3.3,0) -- (a3b);
            \draw[directededge] (a3b) -- (a4);
            \draw[directededge] (a4) -- (a5);
            \draw[directededge] (a5) -- (0.449*6.7,0);
            \draw[thick,dotted] (a5) -- (a5b);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*7.3,0) -- (a5b);
            \draw[directededge] (a5b) -- (a6);
        \end{scope}

        \begin{scope}[yshift=-2cm]
            \node[] at (-0.449,0) {$D_i:$};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_0$] (a1) at (0,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_1$] (a2) at (0.449,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_2$] (a3) at (0.449*2,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-2}$] (a3b) at (0.449*4,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-1}$] (a4) at (0.449*5,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i}$] (a5) at (0.449*6,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{n-1}$] (a5b) at (0.449*8,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_n$] (a6) at (0.449*9,0) {};
            
            \node[] at (0.449*10,0) {$\times i$};

                        \draw[directededge] (a2) -- (a1);
            \draw[directededge] (a3) -- (a2);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*2.7,0) -- (a3);
            \draw[thick,dotted] (a3b) -- (a3);
            \draw[directededge] (a3b)-- (0.449*3.3,0);
            \draw[directededge] (a4) -- (a3b);
            \draw[directededge] (a1) to[out=20, in=160] (a5);
            \draw[directededge] (a5) -- (0.449*6.7,0);
            \draw[thick,dotted] (a5) -- (a5b);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*7.3,0) -- (a5b);
            \draw[directededge] (a5b) -- (a6);
        \end{scope}

        \begin{scope}[yshift=-4cm]
            \node[] at (-0.449,0) {$D_n:$};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_0$] (a1) at (0,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_1$] (a2) at (0.449,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_2$] (a3) at (0.449*2,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-2}$] (a3b) at (0.449*4,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i-1}$] (a4) at (0.449*5,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{i}$] (a5) at (0.449*6,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_{n-1}$] (a5b) at (0.449*8,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$v_n$] (a6) at (0.449*9,0) {};
            
            \node[] at (0.449*10,0) {$\times n-1$};

                        \draw[directededge] (a2) -- (a1);
            \draw[directededge] (a3) -- (a2);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*2.7,0) -- (a3);
            \draw[thick, dotted] (a3b) -- (a3);
            \draw[directededge] (a3b)-- (0.449*3.3,0);
            \draw[directededge] (a4) -- (a3b);
            \draw[directededge] (a5) -- (a4);
            \draw[directededge] (0.449*6.7,0) -- (a5);
            \draw[thick] (a5b) -- (a5);
            \draw[directededge] (a5b) -- (0.449*7.3,0);
            \draw[directededge] (a1) to[out=20,in=160] (a6);
        \end{scope}
    \end{tikzpicture}
    \caption{An illustration of the construction of $H$.}
    \label{fig:quadratic_bound_paths_unb_degree}
\end{figure}

    Note that, by construction, $H$ has $\frac{1}{2}n(n+1)-1$ snapshots, each snapshot of $H$ is a directed path, all vertices except $v_0$ has degree $3$ in $	\ifx\relax H\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}	\fi$ (the neighborhood of $v_j$ being precisely $\{v_{j-1},v_{j+1},v_0\}$), and $v_0$ is adjacent to $v_1$ in every snapshot of $H$.
    
    We now prove that $H$ does not admit any  temporal exploration. Assume for a contradiction that such a temporal exploration exists, so there exists a sequence of vertices
    \[
        (u_0,u_1,\ldots,u_{\ell(H)})
    \]
    where, for every $j\in [\ell(H)]$, either $u_{j-1}=u_j$ or $u_{j-1}u_j \in A(H_j)$ -- where $H_j$ is the $j^{\rm th}$ snapshot of $H$ -- and all vertices of $H$ appear along the sequence. Note that $u_{\ell(H)} = v_n$ since $v_n$ is a sink in every snapshot of $H$.

    For every $i\in [n-1]$, let $f_i = u_{\frac{1}{2}i(i+1)}$, and let $S_i = \{u_j: j\leq \frac{1}{2}i(i+1)\}$.
    Intuitively speaking, the sequence $(u_j)_{j\in [\ell(H)]}$ can be seen as the sequence of moves of an agent exploring $H$. In this case, $f_i$ corresponds to the position of the agent after the sequence of all $D_j$'s, for $j\leq i$, and $S_i$ corresponds to the set of vertices spanned by the agent.
    We obtain the following by induction.

    \begin{claim}
        For every $i\in [n-1]$, each of the following holds:
        \begin{itemize}
            \item if $f_i = v_i$ then $S_i \cap \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_n\}=  \emptyset$;
            \item if $f_i\in \{v_0,v_1,\ldots,v_{i-1}\}$ then $S_i \cap \{v_{i},v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}=  \emptyset$; and
            \item if $f_i\in \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_{n}\}$ then $\{v_0,v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{i}\} \setminus S_i \neq  \emptyset$.
        \end{itemize}
    \end{claim}
    \begin{proofclaim}
        Let us fix $i\in [n]$, and assume that the statement holds for all $i'<i$.
        
        Observe first that, among the $\frac{1}{2}i(i+1)$ first snapshots of $H$ (which are copies of $D_1,\ldots,D_i$), there is no arc from $\{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}$ to $\{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$. Therefore, if $f_i\in \{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$, then $S_i \cap \{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\} = \emptyset$, for otherwise there would be some index $j\leq \frac{1}{2}i(i+1)-1$ such that $u_j \in \{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}$ and $u_{j+1} \in \{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$, a contradiction. This shows the first item of the claim.
        Similarly, among the $\frac{1}{2}i(i+1)$ first snapshots of $H$, there is no arc from $\{v_{i},\ldots,v_n\}$ to $\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{i-1}\}$. The second item follows.

        Henceforth, we assume that $f_i \in \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_{n}\}$. If $i=1$ then $f_1 \notin \{v_0,v_1\}$, so $f_1$ is at distance at least $2$ from $v_0$ in $D_1$. Since there is only one snapshot corresponding to $D_1$,  $v_0 \notin S_i$. 
        Hence, assume that $i>1$. We distinguish the three possible cases for $f_{i-1}$.

        If $f_{i-1} = v_{i-1}$, then observe that $f_i$ is at distance $i+1$ from $v_{i-1}$. Since there are only $i$ copies of $D_i$, $f_i$ is not reachable from $f_{i-1}$ in the snapshots corresponding to $D_i$, a contradiction.

        Else if $f_{i-1} \in \{ v_{0},\ldots,v_{i-2}\}$ then by induction $v_{i-1} \notin S_{i-1}$. Since $v_{i-1}$ is a source in $D_i$, it cannot be spanned from $f_{i-1}$ in the snapshots corresponding to $D_i$. Therefore, $v_{i-1} \notin S_{i}$, and the third item holds.

        Finally, if $f_{i-1} \in \{v_{i},\ldots,v_{n}\}$ then by induction there exists $u\in \{v_0,\ldots,v_{i-1}\} \setminus S_{i-1}$. Since $u$ is not reachable from $v_i$ in $D_i$, it follows that $u\notin S_i$. The claim follows.
    \end{proofclaim}

    We apply the claim above with $i=n-1$. 
    If $f_{n-1}=v_{n-1}$, then $v_n\notin S_n$. Since $v_n$ is at distance $n$ from $v_{n-1}$ in $D_n$, and there are only $n-1$ copies of $D_n$, $v_n$ does not appear along the sequence $(u_0,\dots, u_{\ell(H)})$, a contradiction.
    If $f_{n-1} \in \{v_0,\ldots,v_{n-2}\}$, then $v_{n-1}\notin S_{n-1}$. Since $v_{n-1}$ is a source in $D_n$, $v_{n-1}$ is not spanned by $(u_0,\dots, u_{\ell(H)})$, a contradiction.
    Hence, $f_{n-1} = v_{n}$. Therefore, there exists $u\neq v_n$ such that $u\notin S_{n-1}$, and $u$ is not reachable from $v_n$ is $D_n$. Therefore, $u$ is not spanned by $(u_0,\dots, u_{\ell(H)})$, a contradiction. The lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- claim-1: body_md -->
For every $i\in [n-1]$, each of the following holds:
        \begin{itemize}
            \item if $f_i = v_i$ then $S_i \cap \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_n\}=  \emptyset$;
            \item if $f_i\in \{v_0,v_1,\ldots,v_{i-1}\}$ then $S_i \cap \{v_{i},v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}=  \emptyset$; and
            \item if $f_i\in \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_{n}\}$ then $\{v_0,v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{i}\} \setminus S_i \neq  \emptyset$.
        \end{itemize}
<!-- end claim-1 -->
<!-- proofclaim-1: body_md -->
et us fix $i\in [n]$, and assume that the statement holds for all $i'<i$.
        
        Observe first that, among the $\frac{1}{2}i(i+1)$ first snapshots of $H$ (which are copies of $D_1,\ldots,D_i$), there is no arc from $\{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}$ to $\{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$. Therefore, if $f_i\in \{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$, then $S_i \cap \{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\} = \emptyset$, for otherwise there would be some index $j\leq \frac{1}{2}i(i+1)-1$ such that $u_j \in \{v_{i+1},\ldots,v_n\}$ and $u_{j+1} \in \{v_{1},\ldots,v_i\}$, a contradiction. This shows the first item of the claim.
        Similarly, among the $\frac{1}{2}i(i+1)$ first snapshots of $H$, there is no arc from $\{v_{i},\ldots,v_n\}$ to $\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{i-1}\}$. The second item follows.

        Henceforth, we assume that $f_i \in \{v_{i+1},v_{i+2},\ldots,v_{n}\}$. If $i=1$ then $f_1 \notin \{v_0,v_1\}$, so $f_1$ is at distance at least $2$ from $v_0$ in $D_1$. Since there is only one snapshot corresponding to $D_1$,  $v_0 \notin S_i$. 
        Hence, assume that $i>1$. We distinguish the three possible cases for $f_{i-1}$.

        If $f_{i-1} = v_{i-1}$, then observe that $f_i$ is at distance $i+1$ from $v_{i-1}$. Since there are only $i$ copies of $D_i$, $f_i$ is not reachable from $f_{i-1}$ in the snapshots corresponding to $D_i$, a contradiction.

        Else if $f_{i-1} \in \{ v_{0},\ldots,v_{i-2}\}$ then by induction $v_{i-1} \notin S_{i-1}$. Since $v_{i-1}$ is a source in $D_i$, it cannot be spanned from $f_{i-1}$ in the snapshots corresponding to $D_i$. Therefore, $v_{i-1} \notin S_{i}$, and the third item holds.

        Finally, if $f_{i-1} \in \{v_{i},\ldots,v_{n}\}$ then by induction there exists $u\in \{v_0,\ldots,v_{i-1}\} \setminus S_{i-1}$. Since $u$ is not reachable from $v_i$ in $D_i$, it follows that $u\notin S_i$. The claim follows.
<!-- end proofclaim-1 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lemma:hamiltonian_subcubic_graph}
    For every integer $n\geq 2$, there exists a graph $W_n$ and a set $\{s_1,\dots,s_n\} \subseteq V(W_n)$ such that each of the following holds:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $|V(W_n)| = 7n-6$;
        \item $\Delta(W_n) \leq 3$;
        \item for every $i\in [n]$, $d_{W_n}(s_i) \leq 2$; and
        \item for every $i\in [2,n]$, there exists a Hamiltonian path of $W_n$ with extremities $\{s_1,s_i\}$.
    \end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We define $W_n$ explicitly. Let 
    \[
        V(W_n) = \{s_1\} \cup \bigcup_{i=2}^{n} \{u_i,v_i,w_i,x_i,y_i,z_i,s_i\}
    \]
    and
    \begin{align*}
        E(W_n) = &~ \{s_{i-1}u_i,u_iv_i,v_iw_i, w_ix_i,x_iy_i,y_iz_i, z_is_i, u_iy_i, w_iz_i: 2\leq i \leq n\}\\[0em]
        & \cup \{x_iv_{i+1} : 2\leq i \leq n-1\}\\[0em]
        & \cup \{ x_ns_n\}.
    \end{align*}
    See Figure~\ref{fig:blow_up_gadget} for an illustration. It is straightforward to check that $W_n$ has $7n-6$ vertices, $\Delta(W_n)=3$, and all vertices $s_1,\dots,s_n$ have degree at most $2$ in $W_n$.
    It remains to justify that, for every $i \in [2,n]$, $W_n$ admits a Hamiltonian path going from $s_1$ to $s_i$. Let us fix $i\in [2,n]$.

 $s_{n}$}] (sn) at (0.449*14,0) {};
            
            \draw[thickedge, white] (xm) to[out=45, in=135] (vn);
            \draw[edge, red] (xm) to[out=45, in=135] (vn);
            \draw[edge, lightblue] (sm) to (un);
            \draw[edge, thin] (un) to (vn);
            \draw[edge, red] (vn) -- (wn);
            \draw[edge, red] (wn) -- (xn);
            \draw[edge, thin] (xn) -- (yn);
            \draw[edge, lightblue] (yn) -- (zn);
            \draw[edge, lightblue] (zn) -- (sn);

            
            \draw[edge, thin] (wn) to[out=45, in=135] (zn);
            \draw[thickedge, white] (xn) to[out=45, in=135] (sn);
            \draw[edge, red] (xn) to[out=45, in=135] (sn);
            
            \draw[thickedge, white] (um) to[out=45, in=135] (ym);
            \draw[edge, lightblue] (um) to[out=45, in=135] (ym);

        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{An illustration of $W_n$. 
        A Hamiltonian path of $W_n$ going from $s_1$ to $s_i$ is highlighted in red (from $s_1$ to $s_n$) and blue (from $s_n$ to $s_i$). For the sake of better readability of indices, in the figure we let $h=i-1$, $j = i+1$, $k=i+2$, and $l=n-1$.}
        \label{fig:blow_up_gadget}
    \end{figure}
    
    For every $j\in [2,n]$ let $P_j$ be the path $(s_{j-1},u_j,v_j,w_j,x_j,y_j,z_j,s_j)$, and let $Q_j$ be the concatenation $Q_j = P_2 \cdot P_3 \cdot \ldots \cdot P_j$,
    so $Q_j$ is a path from $s_1$ to $s_i$ that spans 
    \[
    \{s_1\} \cup \{u_k,v_k,w_k,x_k,y_k,z_k,s_k : 2\leq k \leq j \}.
    \]
    Note that, if $i=n$ then $Q_n$ is the desired Hamiltonian path. Henceforth, assume $i\in [2,n-1]$. Let $R$ be the path going from $x_i$ to $x_n$ consisting of the concatenation
    \[
        (x_i,v_{i+1},w_{i+1},x_{i+1}) \cdot (x_{i+1},v_{i+2},w_{i+2},x_{i+2}) \cdot \ldots \cdot (x_{n-1}, v_n, w_n, x_n)
    \]
    and $R'$ be the path going from $s_n$ to $s_i$ consisting of the concatenation
    \[
        (s_n,z_n,y_n,u_n,s_{n-1}) \cdot (s_{n-1},z_{n-1},y_{n-1},u_{n-1},s_{n-2}) \cdot \ldots \cdot (s_{i+1},z_{i+1},y_{i+1},u_{i+1},s_{i}).
    \]
    Now, 
    \[
        Q_{i-1} \cdot (s_{i-1}, u_i,v_i,w_i,z_i,y_i,x_i) \cdot R \cdot (x_n,s_n) \cdot R'
    \]
    is a Hamiltonian path of $W_n$ going from $s_1$ to $s_n$. The lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
We assume that $n'\geq 2$.
    Let $H=(H_i)_{i\in [t]}$, $t=\frac{1}{2}n'(n'+1)-1$, be a temporal digraph on $n'+1$ vertices with vertex set $\{v_0,\dots,v_{n'}\}$ as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:lower_bound_path_apex}. Let $W_{n'}$ and $\{s_1,\dots,s_{n'}\}$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lemma:hamiltonian_subcubic_graph}. For every $j\in [2,n']$, let $P_j$ be a Hamiltonian path of $W_{n'}$ with extremities $\{s_1,s_j\}$. We let $\vec{P_j}$ be the orientation of $P_j$ corresponding to a directed path going from $s_1$ to $s_j$, and $\reflectbox{\ensuremath{\vec{\reflectbox{\ensuremath{P_j}}}}}$ be the opposite orientation.
    
    We let $D$ be the temporal digraph with vertex set 
    \[
        V(D) = (V(H) \setminus v_0) \cup V(W_{{n'}}), 
    \]
    where each snapshot $D_i$, $1\leq i\leq \frac{1}{2}n'(n'+1)$ is obtained from $H_i - v_0$ by adding the arcs of the directed path:
    \[
    \begin{cases}
        \reflectbox{\ensuremath{\vec{\reflectbox{\ensuremath{P_n}}}}} \cdot (s_1v_1) &\text{if $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^-_{H_i}(v_0)} = \emptyset$ and $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{H_i}(v_0)}= \{v_1\}$;}\\[0em]
        (v_1,s_1) \cdot \vec{P_n} \cdot &\text{if $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^-_{H_i}(v_0)} = \{v_1\}$ and $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{H_i}(v_0)}= \emptyset$;}\\[0em]
        (v_j,s_j) \cdot \reflectbox{\ensuremath{\vec{\reflectbox{\ensuremath{P_j}}}}} \cdot (s_1v_1) &\text{if $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^-_{H_i}(v_0)} = \{v_j\}$ and $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{H_i}(v_0)}= \{v_1\}$; or}\\[0em]
        (v_1,s_1) \cdot \vec{P_j} \cdot (s_jv_j) &\text{if $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^-_{H_i}(v_0)} = \{v_1\}$ and $\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{H_i}(v_0)}= \{v_j\}$.}
    \end{cases}
    \]
    Note that one of the four cases above holds by choice of $H$. 
    
    By construction, the underlying graph of $D$ is included in the graph obtained from $W_{n} \cup (	\ifx\relax H\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}{\@empty{U}( H )}	\fi-v_0)$ by adding the matching $\{s_1v_1,s_2v_2,\ldots,s_{n'}v_{n'}\}$. Hence, $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi$ has maximum degree $3$, and every snapshot of $D$ is a directed path. Moreover, $D$ does not admit any temporal exploration, as any temporal exploration of $D$ would yield a temporal exploration of $H$ (by replacing any occurrence of a vertex $u\in V(W_n)$ with $v_0$).
    Note that $D$ has $n=8n'-6$ vertices, and that 
    \[
        \ell(D') = \tfrac{1}{2}n'(n'+1) \geq \tfrac{1}{128} n^2,
    \]
    as desired. The result follows.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:underlying_cycle}
    Let $D$ be an always-unilateral temporal digraph of order $n\geq 2$ with $\Delta(	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi)\leq 2$. If $\ell(D)\geq 2n-3$ then $D$ admits a temporal exploration.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
The result is trivial for $n=2$, so let us assume $n\geq 3$. Since every snapshot of $D$ is unilateral, $	\ifx\relax D\relax		\@empty{U}	\else		\mathchoice{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}{\@empty{U}( D )}	\fi$ must be connected, so there exists a labeling $v_0,\dots,v_{n-1}$ of $V(D)$ such that every snapshot is an orientation of a subgraph of the cycle $(v_0,\dots,v_{n-1},v_0)$.

    Note that each snapshot of $D$ contains a Hamiltonian directed path. Up to removing an edge, we thus assume that each snapshot of $D$ consists exactly in a Hamiltonian directed path. 
    By directional duality, we assume that $n-1$ snapshots of $D$ consist of a forward directed path, that is, consist of the directed path 
    \[
    Q_i = (v_i,v_{i+1},\ldots,v_{n-1},v_0,\ldots,v_{i-1})
    \]
    for some $i\in [0,n-1]$. We show that the temporal digraph $H=(H_i)_{i\in [n-1]}$ consisting exactly of $n-1$ such snapshots has a temporal exploration, hence implying that $D$ admits one as well.

    For $i\in [0,n-1]$, let $(u_1^i,u_2^i,\ldots,u_n^i)$ be the labeling of $V(D)$ along $Q_i$. 
    
    Note that, for every $j\in [n]$, and any pair of distinct integers $0\leq i< i'\leq n-1$, we have $u_j^i \neq u_j^{i'}$.
    In particular, for every $j\in [2,n]$, since each snapshot of $H$ consists exactly of the directed cycle $(v_0,v_1,\ldots,v_{n-1},v_0)$ minus one arc, there exists exactly one integer $i\in [0,n-1]$ such that
    \[
        u_{j-1}^iu_{j}^i \notin A(H_j).
    \]
    Hence, by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists $i\in [0,n-1]$ such that, for every $j\in [2,n]$,
    \[
        u_{j-1}^iu_{j}^i \in A(H_j).
    \]
    It follows that $Q_i$ is a temporal exploration of $H$. The result follows.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lemma:directed_king}
	Let \(D=(D_1,D_2)\) be a semicomplete temporal digraph.
	There exists \(v \in 	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi\)
	such that \(	\ifx\relax D\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}{V( D )}	\fi \subseteq 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{2}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{2}^{D}( v )}{R_{2}^{D}( v )}{R_{2}^{D}( v )}{R_{2}^{D}( v )}	\fi\). Moreover, if $v$ is the only such vertex, then $v$ is a source in both $D_1$ and $D_2$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
For every vertex $v\in V(D)$, let us denote by $L(v) = V(D) \setminus 	\ifx\relax v\relax		R_{}^{D}	\else		\mathchoice{R_{}^{D}( v )}{R_{}^{D}( v )}{R_{}^{D}( v )}{R_{}^{D}( v )}	\fi$ the set of vertices that $v$ cannot reach in $D$. Since $D$ is a semicomplete temporal digraph, note that $L(v)$ contains all vertices $u\in V(D)$ such that:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item $u \to_1 v$,
        \item $u \to_2 v$, and
        \item for every vertex $w$, if $v \to_1 w$, then $u \to_2 w$.
    \end{itemize}
    By definition, observe that for any pair of distinct vertices $\{u,v\}$, if $u\in L(v)$, then $v \notin L(u)$.
    Let $H$ be the auxiliary digraph on vertex set $V(D)$ with arc set 
    \[
        A(H) = \{(u,v) : u\in L(v)\}.
    \]
    By the remark above, $H$ does not contain any pair of opposite arcs. 
    We further have that $H$ is acyclic.
    \begin{claim}
        The oriented graph $H$ is acyclic.
    \end{claim}
    \begin{proofclaim}
        Assume for a contradiction that $H$ contains a directed cycle 
        \[
        C=(v_0,v_1,\dots,v_{r},v_0)
        \]
        for some $r\geq 2$. Let $0\leq i,j \leq r$ be two distinct integers such that $v_i \to_1 v_j$ and, with respect to this property, $(i-j) \bmod (r+1)$ is minimum. 
        By relabeling the vertices along $C$, we may assume without loss of generality that $i=0$.
    
        Note that, by construction of $C$, for every $0\leq k\leq r$ we have that $v_{k} \in L(v_{k+1})$, which by definition implies that $v_{k}\to_{1} v_{k+1}$ and $v_{k}\to_{2} v_{k+1}$ (with indices taken modulo $r+1$). In particular, we have $j\neq r$, and we now distinguish two possible cases.
    
        Assume first that $j=r-1$. Then, by definition of $i,j$ we have $v_0 \to_1 v_{r-1}$. Similarly, since $v_{r-1} \in L(v_{r})$, we have $v_{r-1} \to_2 v_r$. Therefore, $v_0\to_1 v_{r-1} \to_2 v_{r}$, hence $v_r$ is reachable from $v_0$ in $D$, a contradiction.
    
        Assume then that $1\leq j\leq r-2$. By choice of the arc $v_0v_j$, observe that $v_{j+1} \to_1 v_{r}$. Since $v_{j} \in  L(v_{j+1})$, it follows that $v_j \to_2 v_{r}$. Therefore, $v_0 \to_1 v_j \to_2 v_{r}$, which contradicts $v_{r} \in L(v_0)$. The claim follows.
    \end{proofclaim}

    It follows from the claim above that $H$ admits at least one source $v$. By construction, $L(v) = \ensuremath{\@empty{N}^-_{H}(v)} = \emptyset$, which means that $v$ can temporally reach every other vertex.
    This shows the first part of the statement.

    As for the second part, assume for a contradiction that $v$ is the unique such vertex (that is, $v$ is the unique source of $H$) and that $v$ is not a source in at least one of $(D_1,D_2)$. Let $u\in V(D)$ be such that $u\to_1 v$ or $u \to_2 v$. Since $H$ is acyclic and $v$ is its unique source, there exists in $H$ a directed path 
		\(
        P=(v_0, v_1,\dots, v_r)
		\)
    going from $v=v_0$ to $u = v_r$. Let $0\leq i<j\leq r$ be two indices such that $v_j \to_1 v_i$ or $v_j \to_2 v_i$ and, with respect to this property, $j-i$ is minimum. The existence of $i,j$ is guaranteed as $u\to_1 v$ or $u \to_2 v$ by assumption. Moreover, by construction of $P$, $j\geq i+2$, as for every $i\in [0,r-1]$ we have $v_{i} \in L(v_{i+1})$, which in particular implies $v_i \to_1 v_{i+1}$ and $v_i \to_2 v_{i+1}$.

    Now, if $v_j \to_1 v_i$ then by choice of $i,j$ we have $v_j\to_1 v_i \to_2 v_{j-1}$, a contradiction, as $v_{j-1} \in L(v_j)$.
    Otherwise, $v_j \to_2 v_i$, and by choice of $i,j$ we have $v_{i+1} \to_1 v_j \to_2 v_i$, a contradiction, as $v_{i} \in L(v_{i+1})$. The statement follows.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- claim-2: body_md -->
The oriented graph $H$ is acyclic.
<!-- end claim-2 -->
<!-- proofclaim-2: body_md -->
ssume for a contradiction that $H$ contains a directed cycle 
        \[
        C=(v_0,v_1,\dots,v_{r},v_0)
        \]
        for some $r\geq 2$. Let $0\leq i,j \leq r$ be two distinct integers such that $v_i \to_1 v_j$ and, with respect to this property, $(i-j) \bmod (r+1)$ is minimum. 
        By relabeling the vertices along $C$, we may assume without loss of generality that $i=0$.
    
        Note that, by construction of $C$, for every $0\leq k\leq r$ we have that $v_{k} \in L(v_{k+1})$, which by definition implies that $v_{k}\to_{1} v_{k+1}$ and $v_{k}\to_{2} v_{k+1}$ (with indices taken modulo $r+1$). In particular, we have $j\neq r$, and we now distinguish two possible cases.
    
        Assume first that $j=r-1$. Then, by definition of $i,j$ we have $v_0 \to_1 v_{r-1}$. Similarly, since $v_{r-1} \in L(v_{r})$, we have $v_{r-1} \to_2 v_r$. Therefore, $v_0\to_1 v_{r-1} \to_2 v_{r}$, hence $v_r$ is reachable from $v_0$ in $D$, a contradiction.
    
        Assume then that $1\leq j\leq r-2$. By choice of the arc $v_0v_j$, observe that $v_{j+1} \to_1 v_{r}$. Since $v_{j} \in  L(v_{j+1})$, it follows that $v_j \to_2 v_{r}$. Therefore, $v_0 \to_1 v_j \to_2 v_{r}$, which contradicts $v_{r} \in L(v_0)$. The claim follows.
<!-- end proofclaim-2 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemma:exploration_K3}
	Let $D=(D_1,D_2)$ be a semicomplete temporal digraph of order $3$. If $D$ does not admit a temporal exploration, then both $D_1$ and $D_2$ are isomorphic to $\vec{C_3}$ and $A(D_1) \cap A(D_2) = \emptyset$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let us denote $V(D) = \{x,y,z\}$.
    Assume first that $D_1$ is not isomorphic to $\vec{C_3}$. Since $D_1$ is semicomplete, in particular it contains a vertex, say, $x$, with out-degree $2$.
    Since $D_2$ is semicomplete, there is at least one arc between $z$ and $y$ in $D_2$, say, $yz\in A(D_2)$ without loss of generality. Then $(x,y,z)$ is a temporal exploration of $D$.

    Symmetrically, if $D_2$ is not isomorphic to $\vec{C_3}$, then it contains a vertex, say, $x$, with in-degree $2$, and there is an arc between $y$ and $z$ in $D_1$, say, $zy\in A(D_1)$. Then $(z,y,x)$ is a temporal exploration of $D$.

    Assume now that both $D_1$ and $D_2$ are isomorphic to $\vec{C_3}$. Without loss of generality, assume that $D_1$ has arc set $\{xy,yz,zx\}$. Then either $A(D_2) = A(D_1)= \{xy,yz,zx\}$ or $A(D_2) = \{xz,zy,yx\}$. In the former case, $(x,y,z)$ is a temporal exploration of $D$, and in the latter case $A(D_1) \cap A(D_2) = \emptyset$. The statement follows.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lemma:tournament-exploration-upper-bound}
    Let $D=(D_i)_{i\in [4]}$ be a semicomplete temporal digraph of order $n\geq 3$. There exists a temporal path $P$ of length $2$ with terminal vertex $v^\star$ such that one of the following holds:
    \begin{alphaenumerate}
        \item $P$ is a temporal path of $D[1:2]$ and $v^\star$ can reach every vertex of $V(D) \setminus V(P)$ in $D[3:4]$; or
        \label{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1}
        \item $P$ is a temporal path of $D[1:3]$ and $v^\star$ dominates $V(D) \setminus V(P)$ in $D_4$.
        \label{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:2}
    \end{alphaenumerate}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By \cref{lemma:directed_king}, there exists a vertex $v$ which can temporally reach every vertex of $V$ in $D[3:4]$. Among all such vertices we pick $v$ with largest out-degree in $D_{3}$. 
    By applying \cref{lemma:directed_king} twice, we let $u$ and $w$ be two distinct vertices of $D$ such that both $u$ and $w$ can temporally reach every vertex of $V\setminus \{u,v,w\}$ in $D[3:4]$. We let $S = V\setminus \{v,u,w\}$.

    Let $D' = D-S = (D_1',D_2',D_3',D_4')$. 
		If $D'[1:2]$ admits a temporal exploration $P$, then $P$ satisfies~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1} and we are done. Therefore, we can assume that $D'[1:2]$ does not admit such a temporal exploration. By \cref{lemma:exploration_K3} and by symmetry of the roles of $u$ and $w$, we may assume without loss of generality that $A(D_1') = \{vu,uw,wv\}$ and $A(D_2') = \{vw, wu, uv\}$.

    Assume first that, for some $s\in S$, $s\to_1 u$. Then $s\to_1u\to_2v$ yields a temporal path satisfying~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1}. Henceforth, we thus assume the $u \to_1 s$ for every $s\in S$.
    Similarly, if for some $s\in S$, $s\to_2 v$, then $u\to_1s\to_{2}v$ yields a temporal path satisfying~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1}. Therefore, we further assume that $v\to_2 s$ for every $s\in S$.

    It follows from the observation above that, for every $s\in S$, there exists a temporal path $w\to_1 v\to_2 s$. Therefore, if there exists $s\in S$  that can temporally reach every vertex in $V\setminus \{v,w\}$ in $D[3:4]$, then $w\to_1 v\to_2 s$ is a temporal path satisfying~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1}. We may thus assume that $u$ is the unique vertex that can temporally reach every vertex of $V\setminus \{v,w\}$ in $D[3:4]$. By the second part of \cref{lemma:directed_king}, it follows that $u\to_3 s$ and $u\to_4 s$ for every vertex $s\in S$.

    Assume now that $v \to_3 u$. Then $w\to_{1}v\to_{3}u$ yields a temporal path of $D$ satisfying~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:2}, as $u$ dominates $S$ in $D_4$. Similarly, if $w\to_3 u$
    then $v\to_{2}w\to_{3}u$ yields a temporal path of $D$ satisfying~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:2}.
    
    Therefore, we may assume that $u\to_3 v$, $u\to_3 w$, and $v \not\to_3 u$. Since $u$ dominates $S$ in $D_3$, we have that $u$ dominates $V\setminus \{u\}$ in $D_3$. In particular, $u$ can temporally reach every vertex in $V$ in $D[3:4]$. Moreover, 
    \[
        |\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_3}(u)}| = |V(D)|-1 > |\ensuremath{\@empty{N}^+_{D_3}(v)}|,
    \]
    where the last inequality follows from $v \not\to_3 u$.
    This contradicts the choice of $v$, and concludes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
We proceed by induction on $n$. The result is trivial when $n=2$, and it follows easily from \cref{lemma:exploration_K3,lemma:tournament-exploration-upper-bound} when $n\in \{3,4\}$. We thus assume $n\geq 5$. 
    
		Let $D=(D_i)_{i ∈ [t]}$.
		 Let $P$ be a temporal path of $(D_1,D_2,D_3,D_4)$ with terminal vertex $v^\star$ guaranteed by Lemma~\ref{lemma:tournament-exploration-upper-bound}, and let $D' = D[5:t] - 	\ifx\relax P\relax		V	\else		\mathchoice{V( P )}{V( P )}{V( P )}{V( P )}	\fi$. Note that $t-4 \geq \lfloor \frac{4}{3}(n-3)-1 \rfloor = \lfloor \frac{4}{3}|V(D')|-1 \rfloor$ and that $n-3\geq 2$. Hence, by induction, $D'$ admits a temporal exploration $Q$. We let $u$ be the initial vertex of $Q$. If $P$ satisfies~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:2}, then $v^\star$ dominates $u$ in $D_4$, and the concatenation of $P$ and $Q$ is a temporal exploration of $D$. Otherwise, $P$ satisfies~\cref{enum:tournament-exploration-upper-bound:1}, and there exists a temporal path $P'$ from $v^\star$ to $u$ in $D[3:4]$, and the concatenation of $P$, $P'$, and $Q$ is an exploration of $D$. The result follows.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Let $n\geq 2$ be an arbitrary integer and let $k,r\in \mathbb{N}$ be such that $n=3k+r$ and $r \leq 2$. Let $t=4k+r-2 = \lfloor \frac{4}{3}n-2\rfloor$.
    
    We define a temporal tournament $D=(D_i)_{i\in [t]}$ as follows, see Figure~\ref{fig:lower_bound_tournament} for an illustration.
		Let $X=\{x_1,\dots,x_r\}$ be a set of $r$ vertices ($X$ is empty if $r=0$) and, for every $j\in [k]$, let $U_j = \{u_j,v_j,w_j\}$. Let $U = \bigcup_{j=1}^k U_j$. We define the vertex set of $D$ as
    $V(D) = X \cup U$,
    so $D$ has order exactly $n$. Then, for every $i\in [t]$ we let $D_i$ contain the arcs of
    \begin{align*}
        \{x_jx_\ell : 1\leq j < \ell\leq r\}
        & \cup \{ x_j u : 1\leq j \leq r \text{~~and~~} u\in U\}\\[0em]
        & \cup \{yz : y\in U_j \text{~~and~~} z\in U_\ell \text{~~for some $j<\ell$}\}.
    \end{align*}
    Then, if $i$ is even, we let $D_i$ contain the arcs
    $\bigcup_{j=1}^k \{u_jv_j, v_jw_j, w_ju_j\}$,
    and, if $i$ is odd, we let $D_i$ contain the arcs 
    $\bigcup_{j=1}^k \{v_ju_j, w_jv_j, u_jw_j\}$. This completes the description of $D$.

    \begin{figure}
        \centering
        \begin{tikzpicture}
        \tikzset{bigvertex/.style = {shape=circle,dashed, draw, outer sep=2pt}}
            \begin{scope}
            \node[vertex, label=below:$x_1$] (x1) at (-0.5,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$x_2$] (x2) at (0.5,0) {};
            \begin{scope}[xshift=2.5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u1) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v1) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w1) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_1$] (U1) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}
            \begin{scope}[xshift=5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u2) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v2) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w2) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_2$] (U2) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}
            \begin{scope}[xshift=7.5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u3) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v3) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w3) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_3$] (U3) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}

            \draw[directededge] (U1) to (U2);
            \draw[directededge] (U2) to (U3);
            \draw[directededge] (U1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3);
            
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to (x2);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to (U1);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U2);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U3);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U2);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U3);
            
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U1);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U2.150);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3.150);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U1);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U2.150);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3.150);

            \draw[directededge] (u1) -- (v1);
            \draw[directededge] (v1) -- (w1);
            \draw[directededge] (w1) -- (u1);
            
            \draw[directededge] (u2) -- (v2);
            \draw[directededge] (v2) -- (w2);
            \draw[directededge] (w2) -- (u2);
            
            \draw[directededge] (u3) -- (v3);
            \draw[directededge] (v3) -- (w3);
            \draw[directededge] (w3) -- (u3);
            \end{scope}

            \begin{scope}[yshift=-2.8cm]
            \node[vertex, label=below:$x_1$] (x1) at (-0.5,0) {};
            \node[vertex, label=below:$x_2$] (x2) at (0.5,0) {};
            \begin{scope}[xshift=2.5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u1) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w1) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v1) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_1$] (U1) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}
            \begin{scope}[xshift=5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u2) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w2) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v2) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_2$] (U2) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}
            \begin{scope}[xshift=7.5cm]
                \node[vertex] (u3) at (90:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (w3) at (-150:0.4) {};
                \node[vertex] (v3) at (-30:0.4) {};
                \node[bigvertex, fit=(u1)(v1)(w1), inner sep=1pt, label=below:$U_3$] (U3) at (0,0) {};
            \end{scope}

            \draw[directededge] (U1) to (U2);
            \draw[directededge] (U2) to (U3);
            \draw[directededge] (U1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3);
            
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to (x2);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to (U1);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U2);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U3);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U2);
            \draw[directededge] (x2) to[out=40, in=150] (U3);
            
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U1);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U2.150);
            \draw[directededgeblank] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3.150);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U1);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U2.150);
            \draw[directededge] (x1) to[out=30, in=150] (U3.150);

            \draw[directededge] (u1) -- (v1);
            \draw[directededge] (v1) -- (w1);
            \draw[directededge] (w1) -- (u1);
            
            \draw[directededge] (u2) -- (v2);
            \draw[directededge] (v2) -- (w2);
            \draw[directededge] (w2) -- (u2);
            
            \draw[directededge] (u3) -- (v3);
            \draw[directededge] (v3) -- (w3);
            \draw[directededge] (w3) -- (u3);
            \end{scope}
        \end{tikzpicture}
        \caption{The construction of $D_i$ for $n=11$ with $i$ being even (top) and odd (bottom). Arcs to or from a set \(U_i\) in the picture represent arcs incident to all vertices inside said \(U_i\).}
        \label{fig:lower_bound_tournament}
    \end{figure}
    
    By construction, in each $D_i$, $X$ dominates $V(D) \setminus X$, and for every $j,\ell \in [k]$, $U_j$ dominates $U_\ell$ for $\ell > j$. Therefore, any temporal exploration  explores $X$ first and then the vertices of $U_1,\dots,U_k$ in this order. 
    Observe that at least $|X|-1$ snapshots are required to explore $X$ and that, for every $j$, at least four snapshots are required to explore $U_j$ (unless $j=1$ and $X=\emptyset$, in which case three snapshots are enough). Therefore, any temporal exploration of $D$ requires at least 
		\(
        r + 4k -1 = t+1
		\)
    snapshots. This shows that $D$ does not admit any temporal exploration.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lemma:tournament_Hamiltonian_prescribed_NP-hard}
    \PrescribedHamiltonianPath/ is \NP/-hard even if $\ell_1 = 1$ and, for every $i\in [r-1]$, $\ell_{i+1} \geq \ell_i + 2$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
We reduce from the {\sc $(3,B2)$-SAT} problem, which is the restriction of $3$-SAT in which every literal appears exactly twice (that is, for every variable $x$, both $x$ and $\overline{x}$ appear exactly twice). This problem has been shown to be \NP/-hard by Berman, Karpinski, and Scott~\cite{Berman2004}.

    Let $(X,\mathcal{C})$ be an instance of $(3,B2)$-SAT, where $X=\{x_1,\dots,x_n\}$ is the set of variables and $\mathcal{C}=\{C_1,\dots,C_m\}$ is the set of clauses. 
		The arbitrary ordering of the clauses $C_1,C_2,\ldots{},C_m$ induces an ordering of the occurrences of each literal $x_i$ and its negation $\overline{x_i}$ in these. 
    We now build a tournament $T$ from $(X,\mathcal{C})$ as follows, see Figure~\ref{fig:reduction_ham_prescribed} for an illustration.

    node[midway, right=8pt] (W) {$W$};
